CLOUD CONSOLE
HARDENING — WAVE 28:
THE INSTRUMENT FIXED
“I COULD NOT LOOK”
AT THE ROOT AND LEFT
IT STANDING AT THE
OBJECT — AND THE
ENVIRONMENT IT RUNS
IN IS THE ONE PLACE
IT CANNOT LOOK
────────────────────

Wave 27 was the
best-argued wave
this epic has run.
It refuted both of
the lead's headline
findings, paid
Standing Law 0 by
hand with 57 re-
parented rows, and
proved that the
instrument
certifying this
epic's health was
manufacturing false
defect reports out
of a directory it
could not read. Five
of its six person-
facing slices are on
main. One PR is open
— #9356 — and it is
the one everything
else reads through.

The lead offered
this wave a binary:
(a) give the CI job
the history the
instrument needs, or
(b) restructure the
red control to
assert clause (a)
alone. Both are
defensible. Both are
also incomplete, and
this Paper's
headline finding is
why: measured live,
in this session, the
WAVE-27-FIXED
predicate — the one
on #9356, with both
of its new refusals
armed — still emits
six verbatim “commit
… is not an ancestor
of origin/main”
sentences inside a
shallow clone. Same
disease, same prose,
one condition over,
in the exact
environment the
instrument runs in
automatically.

is already true
about each item

THE FINDING: wave 27
named the shallow
clone in its own
fix's header
comment, and did not
close it

seal-
predicate.mjs:453 is
one line: `try {
execFileSync('git',
['-C', REPO, 'merge-
base', '--is-
ancestor', commit,
'origin/main'], …);
} catch {
problems.push(`commi
t ${commit} is not
an ancestor of
origin/main`); }`.
The catch is
unconditional.
Charter D318
enumerated exactly
what it swallows —
“not-a-git-repo /
no-origin-main /
shallow-clone /
unknown-sha” — and
wave 27's fix closed
the first two and
wrote the other two
into a comment.

The wave-27 header
comment says it out
loud, verbatim: “the
ancestry `catch` at
the top of
`verifyCommit`
swallows not-a-git-
repo, no
origin/main, a
shallow clone and an
unknown sha into one
identical claim
about the PRODUCT.”
The remedy that
follows checks that
`.github/workflows/c
loud.yml` exists and
that `git rev-parse
--show-toplevel`
resolves to REPO. A
depth-1 CI clone
satisfies both.

Measured live in
this session,
controlled pair,
same live ledger,
same minute, nothing
varied but the
clone:

The instrument that
certifies this epic
reports “I could not
look” as “I found a
defect” — the exact
sin wave 27 fixed —
in the one
environment where it
runs without a human
present. That is not
a test failure. It
is the sixth
standing-test
clause, live, on the
very instrument that
authored it.

THE DIRECTION: one
disease, two
audiences — the
console and its
instrument both stop
asserting what they
did not measure

This wave has one
sentence and it
points in both
directions at once.
A system that could
not determine
something must say
so. It must not
substitute a
plausible guess, a
default, or the
negative branch of a
two-way test it was
unable to run.

Pointed at the
INSTRUMENT, that
sentence resolves
the lead's binary by
refusing it. Option
(a) alone leaves the
predicate lying in
every other shallow
environment — a
container, a fork
PR, an agent's `--
depth 1`, any future
job. Option (b)
alone leaves
standing the fact
the lead correctly
identified as bigger
than the test: that
every seal run under
this workflow is
structurally unable
to evaluate clause
(b). The answer that
pays both is the
DISCRIMINATION:
teach the git legs
to tell rc 1 (“not
an ancestor”, an
honest answer about
the product) from rc
128 / missing object
/ shallow repository
(“I could not look”,
an honest answer
about the
environment). Once
the predicate can
tell those apart,
(b) becomes the
correct shape for
the control — it
asserts the floor it
is actually about —
and (a) becomes a
verified environment
declaration rather
than a workaround,
because a regression
back to depth-1
would then produce
an honest INFRA-
FAULT the job can
assert on.

Pointed at the
PERSON, the same
sentence is already
sitting in the
ledger as open rows.
A refused connection
is reported as “A
network step timed
out. Retry usually
fixes this.” — the
console did not
classify the
failure, so it
guessed, and the
guess carries
ADVICE. A person
switches on
“Deployment failed”
alerts across email
and every chat
channel, and the
event has zero
producers anywhere
in the repository —
the console tells
them they are
subscribed to
something that
cannot arrive. These
are not clipping
bugs. They are the
epic's founding
vision, in its
purest form, and
they are the wave's
person bodies.

What the finished
wave looks like

• #9356 is MERGED,
  first and alone,
  and it merged
  because its
  control was
  restructured to
  assert clause (a)
  — the floor it
  exists to test —
  not because
  anything red was
  weakened. Nothing
  about the depth-1
  environment
  changed to get it
  green.
• The seal predicate
  has a THIRD
  refusal in the
  same discipline as
  its first two:
  HISTORY-
  UNAVAILABLE. Run
  it in a shallow
  clone and it exits
  2 with a named
  code and zero
  invented findings,
  exactly as it now
  does in a git-
  archive
  extraction.
  Mutation-proved
  with a REAL `git
  clone --depth 1`,
  not a stub — the
  wave-27 lesson is
  that stubbing the
  leg only moves the
  failure.
• console-
  harness.yml stops
  being an
  environment the
  instrument cannot
  work in. The seal
  job declares the
  history it needs,
  and a self-check
  reds if that
  declaration
  silently reverts —
  a guard on the
  guard's
  environment, which
  is the class of
  defect this whole
  wave is about.
• The D307 door
  guard is ARMED,
  not ruled on for a
  third wave.
  `surface` becomes
  an enum; the open
  rows are
  backfilled BY THE
  LEAD'S HAND at
  Decide (wave 27
  proved that
  pattern with 57
  re-parents); the
  fence widening is
  a numbered charter
  decision written
  before a builder
  touches
  mutations.ex; and
  the refusal is
  scoped to this
  epic's parent_id
  so its blast
  radius is this
  epic, not the
  whole task system.
• At least four —
  target five —
  slices where a
  PERSON is
  misinformed or
  cannot reach a
  control, led by
  the two rows where
  the console gives
  a confident wrong
  answer to a
  question it never
  asked.

What this wave
deliberately does
NOT do

• It does not spend
  a slice on
  `.preview-url`.
  That premise
  failed smoke:
  #9357 already
  covers it by
  inheritance and
  the committed CSS
  comment says so.
  Booking it would
  be paying twice
  for one fix.
• It does not chase
  orphans to zero.
  57 is stated with
  all three
  provenance facts
  and repaid only by
  rows that leave
  honestly. Wave 27
  already made the
  largest Law-0
  payment in the
  epic's history; a
  wave spent on
  ledger hygiene is
  the disease the
  charter names at
  waves 9/10/11.
• It does not touch
  .github/required-
  checks.json, and
  it registers
  nothing.

The direction
debate: three
candidates, argued,
one committed

Rival A — “Finish
the instrument,
build the door” (the
wish read literally)

Take the lead's
binary, pick one
leg, and spend the
rest of the wave on
the D307 door guard
as a full multi-
slice build: the
`surface` enum, the
backfill, the fence
ruling, the refusal,
the mutation proof.
Clean, obedient, and
it retires the
highest-leverage row
in the epic.

WHY IT LOSES. Three
counts. First,
arithmetic: four
instrument slices
against a floor of
four person bodies
means eight slices
minimum, and this
epic's own history
says a wave that
spends 4-of-6 on
instruments moves
the row count the
wrong way (waves
9/10/11: +11, +16,
+7). Second, the
door guard's
dependency chain —
enum → backfill →
fence ruling →
refusal — is SERIAL
and cannot fan out
to parallel
builders, so it
converts a fan-out
wave into a queue.
Third and worst, it
accepts the false
binary. Picking (a)
or (b) at speed
leaves the predicate
lying in every
shallow environment
that is not this one
workflow, and this
Paper has now proved
live that such an
environment exists
and that the epic's
own instrument is in
it.

Rival B — “Pay Law 0
to zero and drive at
the seal”

orphans is 57 and
a=FAIL is the only
clause failing.
Spend the wave
closing, forwarding
and adjudicating
residue — including
the cch-w22-s7 split
D325 already ruled —
and get the epic
within sight of an
honest SEAL.

WHY IT LOSES. It is
the epic's stated
disease wearing a
productivity
costume. The
charter's vision is
that the CONSOLE
stops lying; a wave
with zero person
bodies fails the
ratio outright and
would be the fourth
restatement of a law
rather than a
payment of it. It
also mistakes the
seal for the goal —
D318 proved the
predicate could SEAL
an epic that does
not exist, which
means a falling
orphan count was
never the thing
worth optimising.
HARVESTED ANYWAY:
this Paper quotes
the reading with all
three provenance
facts, and the cch-
w22-s7 adjudication
is booked as a
Decide-phase ledger
action for the lead,
costing zero
builders.

Rival C — “One
disease, two
audiences” —
COMMITTED

Two instrument
slices that pay the
environment question
properly plus the
discrimination that
generalises it; one
door-guard slice
made buildable by
moving its backfill
dependency onto the
lead's hands at
Decide; and four-to-
five person bodies
chosen so that at
least two of them
are literally the
same defect the
instrument has — a
system asserting a
fact it did not
measure.

WHY IT WINS. It is
the only candidate
where the instrument
work and the person
work are the SAME
argument, which
means the wave's
finding transfers:
the sentence that
fixes the seal
predicate is the
sentence a builder
applies to
`connection
refused`. It keeps
the ratio at four-
plus without
inventing filler. It
refuses the lead's
binary on evidence
rather than on
preference — the
depth-1 run is
measured, not
reasoned. And it
ships the door guard
ARMED this wave by
removing the
dependency that
blocked it, rather
than ruling on it
for a third
consecutive wave.

The sharpest attack
on the winner, and
how the wave absorbs
it

“You are deferring
the D307 door guard
AGAIN. Wave 26 ruled
on it, wave 27 filed
it, and ‘build the
enum, defer the
refusal’ is exactly
the shape that lets
wave 29 defer it a
fourth time.
Meanwhile you have
talked yourself into
a THIRD instrument
slice nobody asked
for.”

TAKEN, and it
changes the plan
rather than being
argued away. The
wave does not ship
the enum and defer
the refusal — it
ships the refusal
ARMED, and it
removes the blocker
by re-classifying
it. D324 gave four
honest reasons the
guard could not
ship: (i) no
classifier, (ii) the
fence widening is
not a builder's
call, (iii) CI is
post-hoc detection
not refusal, (iv)
shipping before the
backfill makes the
guard's first act on
main a refusal of
legitimate filings.
Reasons (i) and (iv)
are LEDGER work, and
wave 27 proved
conclusively that
ledger work belongs
to the lead's hands
at Decide — 57 re-
parents, executed in
minutes, with the
post-condition
asserted on the
destination roster.
Reason (ii) is a
numbered charter
decision, which is
also a Decide-phase
artifact. Reason
(iii) argues FOR the
door, not against
it. So the builder's
slice is what
remains: the enum
validation and the
refusal, scoped to
one parent_id, with
a mutation proof
that shows it can
both refuse and
pass. If survey
finds the backfill
is larger or messier
than wave 27's re-
parent — a real
risk, since
`surface` is
populated on 24 of
343 rows and is free
prose — the wave
scopes the guard's
population to rows
the backfill
actually reached and
says so, rather than
arming a refusal
over rows it cannot
classify.

On the third
instrument slice: it
is not a third, it
is the CORRECT
first. Without the
discrimination, both
of the lead's
options are
workarounds, and
this Paper has the
run output proving
it. The wave's own
ratio discipline
still binds — if the
discrimination and
the environment
declaration cannot
be shown to be one
coherent slice at
Decide, the
environment half
merges with #9356
and the wave spends
the recovered budget
on a fifth person
body.

Premise smoke —
every inherited
premise this
direction leans on,
run rather than
quoted

Two premises failed
smoke and both are
stated rather than
dropped: `.preview-
url` is already
paid, and “a depth-1
clone has no
origin/main” is
false in the half
that would have
shaped the remedy.
One premise was
ENLARGED: the wrong-
root fix does not
generalise, and the
proof is a live run
rather than an
argument.

The shape the wave
is aiming at — seven
named, five expected

Tentative. Decide
finalises after two
exploration rounds;
survey may kill any
of these and is
expected to promote
at least one
replacement on its
own evidence, as
wave 27's flick-to-
bottom and
progressbar bodies
were.

RATIO: at least four
slices where a
PERSON is
misinformed or
cannot reach a
control. Items 4, 5,
6 and one survey-
promoted body are
the four; the wave
does not ship if
fewer than four
survive.

The survey plan — 17
assignments, and why
each one can change
the plan

Width is deliberate.
Every load-bearing
assumption in the
direction above
appears below as a
question a surveyor
can answer with a
run.

The consequence for
the wave is a
promotion, not a
footnote. The
direction argued the
discrimination fix
from ONE live
measurement of one
instrument. The
survey turned that
into a repo-wide,
cross-language
pattern with a named
control group — and
it supplied the
model arm from the
epic's own code,
four lines below the
defect.

What is now
ESTABLISHED

1. The seal
predicate's two git
legs disagree about
the same failure
class, four lines
apart

On origin/main,
seal-predicate.mjs:4
53-454 catches ANY
throw from merge-
base --is-ancestor
and pushes a
definite product
claim ("commit X is
not an ancestor of
origin/main"). :457-
458 catches the
identical class from
git show and throws
Infra → exit 2
INFRA-FAULT. The
honest arm is
already in the file,
applied to one leg
and not its
neighbour. That is a
far stronger charter
sentence than "add
discrimination to
the git legs", and
it explains why the
lead's stubbing
remedy failed:
stubbing :453 hands
control to :457,
which correctly
raises Infra. The
lead patched the leg
that was already
right.

2. The
discrimination the
direction proposed
is INSUFFICIENT —
proved live, and
this is the survey's
crown

The direction
proposed keying on
rc 1 (product) vs rc
128 / missing object
/ shallow
(environment). A
surveyor built four
real clones and
measured the truth
table. The residual
case breaks it: in a
--depth 1 clone with
the commit object
fetched IN
(grafted), cat-file
-e returns rc 0, git
show --name-only
returns rc 0 with
real bytes — and
merge-base --is-
ancestor returns rc
1 for a commit a
full clone confirms
IS an ancestor.
Object presence is
not the
discriminator; the
truncated WALK is.
Ancestry degrades
before the diff
does.

So the refusal must
key on shallowness
itself (rev-parse --
is-shallow-
repository), on ref
resolution (rev-
parse --verify --
quiet origin/main)
and on object
presence (cat-file -
e <sha>^{commit}) as
three SEPARATE
probes — not on an
exit code alone. A
guard phrased as "is
this a valid git
work tree?" waves
the grafted case
through, which is
exactly wave 27's
shape repeated one
level down.

3. #9356's red is
ONE assertion, and
the workflow already
forbade the shape
that produced it

The failure is seal-
predicate.test.mjs:1
273 —
assert.equal(sealed.
status, SEAL) —
actual 1, expected
0, with the mutant's
own token reading
a=PASS b=FAIL
c=PASS. Clause (a),
the only thing that
control is about,
PASSES in CI. And
console-
harness.yml:239-248
on origin/main
already states,
verbatim, that
depth-1 makes the
predicate
"manufacture a false
NO SEAL out of a
shallow clone" and
that "the test file
is hermetic
instead". Wave 27's
empty-roster control
is the first and
only exit-code-
asserting run in the
file that goes down
the LIVE path. It
violates a contract
the job had written
down.

This flips both of
the lead's options.
Option (b) is not a
dodge — it RESTORES
the file's own
stated invariant.
Option (a) does not
merely change clone
cost: seal-
predicate.test.mjs:3
5 sets REPO =
resolve(HERE,
'../../../..') and
passes --repo REPO
to ~15 spawns, so
the suite is
hermetic today only
by accident —
because depth-1
makes every git read
fail uniformly. Flip
to fetch-depth: 0
and clause (b)
begins returning
real answers about
real epic commits,
and the suite's
verdict silently
becomes a function
of history state
with not one test
line changed. Option
(a) alone converts a
hermetic suite into
a history-coupled
one.

4. Clause (a) has
FOUR unguarded lies
of its own, and one
is a FALSE SEAL

The wave has been
treating clause (b)
as the diseased
half. It is not the
only one.

Two premise
corrections that
come with it.
BP_TOKEN is
decorative on this
endpoint — a bogus
bearer returned HTTP
200 with the full
document set, so
"auth failure" is
not a live failure
mode today. And
truncated-JSON is
already handled
correctly (Infra
with the first 60
chars quoted). Do
not spend a slice on
either.

5. The D307 door
guard is BUILDABLE
but the direction's
in-file precedent is
FALSE, and the
backfill is worse
than feared

The direction said
mutations.ex:470-545
already hosts a
create-time door
guard. It does not.
That range is
ensure_claim_not_dro
pped/4, an UPDATE
fence whose first
clause
(mutations.ex:550)
structurally exempts
every fresh birth —
and whose own header
comment names that
hole. The create
clause at :132-158
has zero guard
hooks; guards exist
on exactly four
clauses
(createOrReplace,
replace, both patch
clauses), and
create/createIfNotEx
ists are unguarded
by ruled intent
(D53: "structurally
incapable of
touching a live
row"). That
rationale is about
clobbering an
existing row and
says nothing about a
FORGED BIRTH. The
D307 guard would be
the first guard ever
wired into the
create clause — a
charter-visible
widening, not an
added line.

What is easier than
advertised: surface
IS visible at the
guard point.
incoming_content(att
rs) already exists
and is already the
argument passed to
both existing
guards, and a
committed test
proves the flat
Sanity envelope is
not an escape. Zero
new plumbing.

What is harder:
surface is not a
declared task field
at all (zero hits in
tasks/schema.ex),
and the identifier
surface is ALREADY a
reserved schema-DSL
keyword meaning
field placement
(body|sidebar)
across every plugin
schema JSON. And the
backfill does not
exist as a
mechanical artifact:
a composite
classifier built
from every
structured field the
ledger carries
scored 6/15 (40%)
blind against the
ledger's own human-
written labels —
worse than the 67%
false-refusal rate
D324 measured on the
drafted predicate.
86 of 230 open rows
(37%) are
undecidable from
every field sampled.

THE POPULATION
NUMBER THE WAVE MUST
QUOTE. surface is
populated on 36 of
471 rows across both
epics and on 15 of
230 OPEN rows. On
the 56 live orphans
specifically it is
populated on 5 and
null on 51 — a guard
armed today over
that population
refuses 91% of
legitimate filings.
D324's "24 of 343"
is not refuted, it
is a DIFFERENT
ROSTER (343 was the
pre-re-parent
census). Quote the
roster definition or
the fraction means
nothing.

A surveyor also
found that two
committed tests PIN
the current hole as
expected behaviour —
mutate_controller_te
st.exs:526 and :542,
the latter
explicitly recording
"RESIDUAL HARM… NO
COMPENSATING CONTROL
SHIPS IN THIS
SLICE". Their
fixtures carry no
parent_id, so a
parent_id-scoped
refusal leaves both
green. A guard
scoped any wider
turns them red, and
one of them is a
test asserting the
absence of the very
guard being shipped.

6. D306 fires on the
wave that wrote it:
the wish's "57 re-
parented rows" is
the ORPHAN count

Re-derived
independently from a
fresh detached
worktree at
origin/main
(resolved root
quoted, non-
shallow): NO-SEAL
a=FAIL b=PASS c=PASS
orphans=57
considering=1
residue=60 (live
59), roster 288
{done 194, open 59,
cancelled 34,
considering 1}.
Against wave 27's
343 {189/119/34/1}
the delta decomposes
exactly: RE-PARENTED
55 (only a re-parent
shrinks the
denominator) and
CLOSED 5 (189→194,
cancelled
unchanged). 119 − 55
− 5 = 59. ✓ 57 is
neither integer — it
is today's orphan
count. Wave 28
quotes 55 and 5.

PROVENANCE
CORRECTION (D319).
The Strategize line
quoted "tree
dfdc31db96b8…". That
is the COMMIT sha.
The tree at that
commit is
3cf1bda5661b6a119d01
7d1c8582fd2a93f356ed
. D319 asks for a
tree sha precisely
because a tree is
content-identity;
labelling the commit
as the tree is a
level-skip of the
kind this epic
polices. Same
object, wrong label
— the reading is
unaffected.

7. The .preview-url
kill SURVIVED
adversarial attack,
on measurement

A surveyor was
tasked to red the
Strategist and could
not. Driven from a
clean detached
worktree at
origin/main, the
committed cruel-
fixture leg W27-
deploy-ref-branch-
bounded measured
20/20 cells clean,
rc 0 across 10
widths × 2 themes,
60 rows, with every
per-cell urlLost
NEGATIVE (worst
margin −76.44px).
The leg is not green
by construction: it
dies if the cruel
branch drops below
40 chars or gains a
break opportunity,
it measures the
inheritance per cell
rather than
asserting it in
prose, and it has a
committed 52-
findings-pre-fix
record. .preview-url
is rendered at
exactly one place
repo-wide and always
inside .deploy-ref.
The slice comes off
the board on
evidence.

But the concern
behind wish item 4
is not dead — it
moved one element
over. .deploy-
detail, the class
app.js paints the
raw server deploy-
stage detail into,
appears in overflow-
guard.mjs exactly
once, in a COMMENT
declaring it OUT of
the measured
surface. Zero
assertions. That is
the shape of "a
guard whose SELECTOR
cannot reach the
defect", written
down by the
instrument itself.

9. Honest negatives
— three places the
wave should NOT
spend

• overflow-guard.mjs
  separates
  environment from
  defect correctly:
  die() is used for
  CDP bring-up and
  measurement
  breakage, distinct
  from the
  failures[] path,
  and its
  navigating-catch
  is loop-bounded
  and throws on
  exhaustion.
• registry.ex's ~20
  `_ -> true|false`
  catch-alls are
  Repo.one() nil-vs-
  1 matches, not
  unknown-to-
  definite
  collapses;
  barkpark_in_team?
  documents its
  fail-closed
  intent.
• app.js's catch-to-
  null totalisers:
  the one consumer
  traced
  (presenceSlotHtml)
  renders "Roster
  unreachable" —
  honest about not
  knowing. The other
  two consumers are
  UNTRACED and are a
  verify question,
  not a filed slice.

Also negative on
scope: the
git/history axis is
NOT a class. seal-
predicate.mjs is the
only instrument this
epic owns that execs
git at all. cssom-
parity.mjs weighed
and REJECTED a git-
derived baseline in
committed prose,
naming origin/main-
ref availability
among the contexts
it must survive; all
three path-escape
scripts enumerate
with find citing
D31. Slice 2 stays
single-file. What
generalises is the
DOCTRINE, and it has
two working
implementations
already in-tree:
cssom-parity.mjs:608
(throws on
unreadable input)
and required-checks-
verify.sh (hard-
fails on a
protection read it
cannot make,
deliberately, "an
unreadable input is
a failure and never
a skip").

Corners no surveyor
reached

• What a REAL
  actions/checkout@v
  4 workspace
  contains. Every
  shallow
  measurement in
  this survey is
  from a local `git
  clone --depth 1`
  over file://.
  actions/checkout
  fetches a NARROW
  REFSPEC and a
  merge ref for PRs,
  so CI may present
  the MISSING-REF
  shape (rc 128 on
  origin/main)
  rather than the
  MISSING-OBJECT
  shape measured. A
  guard tested only
  against a depth-
  limited clone
  could still be
  green-by-
  construction on a
  real runner.
• Whether --is-
  shallow-repository
  reports true
  inside a git
  WORKTREE of a
  shallow repo — and
  inside the epic-
  cycle worktrees
  every builder in
  this epic works
  in. If it under-
  reports there, the
  guard is green by
  construction in
  the very place it
  will be built.
• 15+ of the ~20
  Elixir rescue
  sites in
  cloud/lib:
  health.ex:26,
  notifications.ex:4
  36, usage.ex:575-
  659,
  push/jwt.ex:36-
  100,
  sites/deploy.ex:12
  59. Only
  domain_status.ex
  was read in full.
• Task DESCRIPTIONS
  for the 86
  undecidable
  backfill rows. The
  40% classifier
  accuracy is a
  floor on the
  fields SAMPLED,
  not a proof that
  no classifier
  works — this is
  the single
  experiment that
  could turn 'not
  mechanically
  producible' into
  'producible'.
• Whether app.js's
  fleetMutate and
  fetchOffloadTask
  consumers render
  honestly for
  {status: 0, data:
  null}. One traced
  consumer is
  honest; two are
  unchecked.
• js/, web/ and
  webhook payloads
  as consumers of
  deployment_json's
  detail key. Only
  internal/cli was
  checked.
• Whether
  stale_deployment_r
  eaper reaches
  Registry.transitio
  n_deployment_fence
  d/4 or issues its
  own
  Repo.update_all.
  This decides
  whether the
  notification
  dispatch has ONE
  insertion point or
  two.

direction

What Decide cannot
live without knowing

• Whether the CI
  runner's shallow
  shape is MISSING-
  OBJECT or MISSING-
  REF. These need
  different probes,
  and every
  measurement so far
  is from a local
  clone that may
  present the wrong
  one. This decides
  the
  discrimination's
  shape, not just
  its wording.
• Whether --is-
  shallow-repository
  is true inside a
  git worktree of a
  shallow repo —
  because that is
  where the builder
  will test the
  guard.
• Whether the
  clause-(a) lies
  (truncation floor,
  bucket-(c)
  cardinality floor,
  curl --fail) fold
  into the HISTORY-
  UNAVAILABLE slice
  as one coherent
  "the predicate
  stops asserting
  what it did not
  measure" change,
  or whether they
  are a separate
  slice. This is the
  wave's ratio
  pivot: folding
  them recovers a
  person body.
• Whether flipping
  console-unit to
  fetch-depth: 0
  changes any test
  verdict TODAY.
  Cheap to produce
  (run the suite in
  a full clone and a
  depth-1 clone,
  diff per-test
  results) and it IS
  the exact blast
  radius.
• Whether the
  backfill is
  mechanically
  producible from
  task DESCRIPTIONS.
  If yes, the door
  guard arms over a
  real population;
  if no, it arms
  over the rows the
  backfill reached
  and the wave says
  so.
• Whether
  :agent_unreachable
  's :detail is ever
  non-empty at its
  dispatch site —
  this decides
  whether the fifth
  person body is one
  event or two.
• Which function
  hosts the
  deployment_failed
  dispatch so all
  three failure
  seams are covered.

The verify fleet —
12 assignments, all
Opus (Fable
unavailable), 9 of
them RUN something

This is the LAST
look. Every
assignment closes a
named unknown or
proves a load-
bearing claim by
RUNNING it. Nine of
twelve carry a
command; the three
that do not are
genuine lookups.

COVERAGE, INCLUDING
THE NOT-FOUNDS.
Three verifiers did
not open the charter
and said so; v2 did
not open console-
harness.yml and
carried the digest's
reading at its own
authority level; v5
did not re-derive
the clause-(b) axis;
v11 did not re-
verify the deploy-
stage CLASSIFICATION
half; v7 could not
compare row
membership against
the survey's 86-row
undecidable list
because only the
count was reported,
so its 121-vs-86 is
a set-SIZE
comparison over
slightly different
denominators and
must never be
printed as a
subtraction. v1
could not execute on
a real GitHub runner
--
its two CI shapes
are FAITHFUL
RECONSTRUCTIONS of
actions/checkout@v4'
s fetch refspecs,
corroborated by
console-
harness.yml's own
committed comment,
not observations.
Two verifiers'
evidence lives in
throwaway worktrees
and is uncommitted;
their exact output
is quoted in the
slice briefs so a
builder re-authors
rather than re-
derives.

NOT TAKEN, STATED
RATHER THAN DRIFTED:
fetch-depth: 0
(D329); successor-
roster truncation
(D330, structurally
impossible); the
bucket-(c) filter-
drift mode (D330,
refuted); .preview-
url AND .deploy-
detail (D334, both
measured clean, the
second with a
mutation control);
the surface presence
requirement (D331);
the repo-wide `_ ->
[]` sweep (D332 --
four of six
candidates are NOT
the shape and two
are the model arms
to cite). REGISTER
NOTHING;
.github/required-
checks.json is
untouched by every
slice.

The wave plan -- 8
slices, 4 of them
person bodies, 7 in
round 1

FABLE IS UNAVAILABLE
THIS WAVE, so every
slice dispatches on
Opus regardless of
its difficulty axis.
Two slices carry
judgments flip-prone
enough to warrant an
independent second
re-derivation before
merge, and both say
so in their brief.

ANCHOR DISCIPLINE,
the D326 pattern
that avoided wave
27's serial-merge
tax entirely. Three
files are shared and
every claim is
assigned BY NAME:
router.ex -- s5 owns
the `detail:` line
at :10254 and
NOTHING else, s6 is
forbidden it, s8
needs none of it
(site_json/2 already
carries
current_deployment_i
d, verified). app.js
--
s7 owns
domainStageRows/doma
inStages, s8 owns
the siteRow region.
__app.test.mjs -- s7
INSERTS after the
existing
domainStages block,
s8 is the SOLE owner
of the EOF append
point. seal-
predicate.* is
sequenced rather
than shared: s2 does
not dispatch until
s1 is on main.

BACKLOG FILED THIS
WAVE, six rows, all
published under the
epic: cch-w28-bl-
capacity-arm-blames-
hetzner-for-azure (a
bare `quota` token
tells an Azure-
failed person
Hetzner ran out of
servers); cch-w28-
bl-overflow-guard-
clipper-blames-the-
wrong-panel (D322's
latent false
attribution, plus
two further defects
found in the same
six lines -- a 40-
char truncated
dedupe key and a
filler that degrades
to the first panel
in document order);
cch-w28-bl-usage-
meters-say-
unmetered-when-they-
could-not-measure
(the same shape as
D332, weaker,
honestly priced);
cch-w28-bl-w22s7-
split-stamps-never-
landed-and-c9-kill-
is-refuted (the
ledger work, with
corrected anchors
and the unrun
mutation that would
settle C9); cch-w28-
bl-orphans-swallows-
the-disclosed-
considering-row (a
row printed on two
mutually exclusive
lines, plus three
draft twins the
predicate cannot
see); cch-w28-bl-
auto-deploy-refusal-
has-no-event-at-all.

WHAT WOULD FALSIFY
THIS WAVE. If s2's
four probes cannot
be shown to answer
correctly in a REAL
grafted clone and a
REAL simulated
pull_request
checkout -- not a
stub, not a JSON
fixture -- then the
discrimination is a
sentence with an
exit code and s1's
green was bought
rather than earned.
If s4's guard cannot
be shown to REFUSE
and to PASS over
HTTP, and to leave
the two D53 pinning
tests green ONLY
because of its
parent scoping, then
the door was never
armed. And if s7's
`unknown` does not
set terminal:true in
the shipped app.js,
the console keeps
polling a dead
resolver forever and
the person is no
better off than
before -- a server-
only fix here is a
regression wearing a
decision's clothes.

DEBRIEF — the wave
as it will merge,
and the grade it
earned

Seven round-1 slices
were briefed, seven
were built, seven
are green, and all
seven are PUSHED
with a PR open — six
new PRs (#9404-
#9409) plus #9356,
whose head was fast-
forwarded to carry
cch-w28-s1 so the
fix rides the PR it
was written for
rather than
duplicating it. cch-
w28-s2 is round 2 by
design and correctly
sits `open` on the
ledger. The reviewer
re-ran every slice
gate on the FINAL
branch, fixed four
things in place
rather than
reporting them, and
proved the wave
coheres by merging
all seven onto
origin/main in one
probe branch: the
FULL cloud suite is
2693 tests / 0
failures there, and
__app.test.mjs is
797/797.

The four things the
reviewer FIXED
rather than reported

1. THE FALSE
SENTENCE IN THE D307
GUARD'S HEADER
(commit 73438f573).
The shipped comment
claimed that
deleting the scoping
arm takes
mutate_controller_te
st.exs “from 36/36
to 23 failures,
including the D53
create-family pins”.
The builder had
already measured 1
failure and stamped
an honest --miss on
the criterion — but
left the brief's
number standing in
the code. Re-run
independently at
review (delete `not
cch_epic_child?(pare
nt) -> :ok`, change
nothing else): 46
tests / 1 failure,
and that failure is
this slice's own
scoping test. The
comment now states
the measured result
and says plainly
that the scoping is
a DESIGN boundary,
not a load-bearing
leg under today's
corpus. An epic
whose thesis is
“stop asserting what
you did not measure”
cannot ship a header
comment that does
exactly that.

2. `:nxdomain` IS AN
ANSWER, NOT A FAULT
(commits 90ab9999a +
438495a96) — a
REVIEW DECISION
superseding the
letter of D332. The
s7 builder built
D332 as written,
measured that one
fifth of it was
wrong, and filed
task-
3fbfff8c97b50c8f at
priority 0 rather
than quietly re-
deciding. He was
right on both
counts. Re-measured
at review on this
host:
`:inet.getaddrs` of
a non-existent name
returns `{:error,
:nxdomain}` on BOTH
families — which is
precisely what a
freshly attached,
still-propagating
domain looks like
through the real
transport. Shipped
literally, D332
would have turned
the MOST COMMON
waiting state into
“we could not check”
and stripped its
propagation advice:
one lie swapped for
another, in the very
rung the slice
existed to make
honest. NXDOMAIN
means the resolver
ANSWERED. Fixed with
`@answered_empty
[:nxdomain]`, and —
the half that
matters for the
standing test — the
DNS test fakes now
RETURN nxdomain for
a missing record
instead of `{:ok,
[]}`, a shape
production never
produces. task-
3fbfff8c97b50c8f is
closed with the
ruling, its
measurement and its
residual gap on the
record.

3. THE REAP ALERT
CAP IS NOW DRIVEN
(commit ab2a6dce5).
`@reap_alert_cap 25`
decides that the
26th owner in a
cluster incident
hears nothing by
email — a person-
facing SUPPRESSION
that no fixture
crossed. The builder
flagged it against
himself; the review
paid it: a test
fails 27 deployments
in one sweep and
asserts exactly 25
emails plus the
suppression log
line, with
`Registry.reap_alert
_cap/0` exposed so
the test reads the
real constant
instead of a copy.
Mutation-proved:
replacing
`Enum.split(alerts,
@reap_alert_cap)`
with `{alerts, []}`
reds exactly that
test and nothing
else.

4. THE MUTATION
ANCHORS IN s1's
CONTROL (commit
52a80c7be).
`String.prototype.re
place` on an anchor
that has drifted is
a SILENT no-op, and
`mutatedRun`'s own
`notEqual(out, src)`
guard would have
been satisfied by
the roster mutation
alone — so a future
refactor of seal-
predicate.mjs could
have left the floor
mutation un-applied
and the control
passing vacuously.
Each anchor is now
asserted present
before use.

with the fixed
instrument from a
real git tree

Ledger audit

Honest across the
board, with no
corrections owed.
Every built slice is
`in_progress`,
published, with
evidence stamped as
the builder worked
and the merge-gated
criterion left open
for the lead — s1
6/7, s3 6/7, s4 7/8
(criterion 4 an
honest --miss
carrying the
refuting numbers),
s5 7/8, s6 9/10, s7
10/11, s8 7/8. cch-
w28-s2 correctly
sits `open` as a
deferred round-2
slice. Backlog rows
filed by builders
are published and
open. The ONLY
ledger mutation this
review made was
closing task-
3fbfff8c97b50c8f
with the nxdomain
ruling (criteria 0
and 1 stamped with
evidence, criterion
2 recorded as an
honest --miss and
closed over with a
written
criteria_override
rather than graded
by its own closer).

Grade: A−

WHAT EARNS IT. The
wave's headline
question was a
judgment call
between two
defensible options
and it was decided
cleanly, then
executed without
cheating: #9356 is
green in a REAL
depth-1 clone with
the thing that was
red still red-able,
the fabrication
sentences relocated
rather than deleted,
and an unanchored
regex that
discriminated
nothing repaired on
the way past. Three
builders reported
measurements that
CONTRADICTED their
own briefs — the 23-
failure prediction,
the `returning:`
prescription, the
nxdomain
classification — and
each filed the
contradiction
instead of
reinterpreting the
criterion into
something passable.
That is the
behaviour this epic
exists to produce,
and it is why the
review caught real
defects instead of
typos. Four person-
facing bodies landed
with genuine fail-
before proofs, and
the cross-slice
integration is
proved by a 2693/0
full-suite run
rather than
asserted.

WHAT HOLDS IT BACK.
The instrument is
still structurally
blind in the only
place it runs
automatically — s2
was deferred by the
sequenced-rounds
law, which is
correct process, but
it means the epic is
still certified by a
predicate that can
only give a partial
verdict in CI, and
s3 turned that from
an unstated fact
into a DOCUMENTED
one, which by the
wave's own standing
test is a sentence,
not a fix. The D307
door guard shipped
armed but its hard
tier can reach
almost nothing (no
fixture outside its
own block carries a
`surface` key) and
its absence tier is
a warn, so the
filing law still
holds because people
are careful, not
because a door
refuses. s7 shipped
a person-facing
regression in the
most common domain
state that only
review caught — the
builder measured it
and escalated
correctly, but a
wave that ships a
decision it knows
may be wrong is one
review away from
shipping it for
real. And the domain
rail now goes
TERMINAL on
`unknown` with no
re-check affordance,
which trades a
spinning lie for a
frozen truth.

What the next wave
should take

• MERGE ORDER, and
  it matters: round
  1 first (#9356,
  then #9404-#9409,
  which are file-
  disjoint and all
  merge cleanly onto
  main in one
  probe). ONLY THEN
  cch-w28-s2 — it
  rewrites the same
  file #9356
  rewrites and must
  mutate the
  discrimination out
  of #9356's fail-
  before controls.
• cch-w28-s2's hard
  constraint, armed
  as a tripwire by
  s1: HISTORY-
  UNAVAILABLE must
  surface as a
  verdict-token
  LETTER at exit 1,
  never a process-
  level exit-2
  INFRA-FAULT. An
  INFRA-FAULT prints
  a=UNKNOWN and will
  red s1's control —
  deliberately.
• The REQUIRED
  `Console gate`
  depends on
  guerrilla.barkpark
  .cloud being
  reachable (one
  live spawn, test
  56). s3 documented
  it; nobody has
  fixed it. A
  required gate that
  a third-party host
  can red is the
  same defect class
  as a guard no job
  runs.
• The D307 guard's
  promotion: the
  `surface` ENUM
  plus the backfill
  of open rows,
  which is what
  turns the warn
  tier hard. Until
  then “every epic
  row declares a
  surface” is false
  and the code says
  so.
• The s7 residue: a
  re-check
  affordance for a
  TERMINAL `unknown`
  rail, and
  domainRungChip's
  missing `unknown`
  arm (task-
  1daff7bc1bf46ceb)
  — a screen reader
  is still told
  “waiting” one
  layer below a rung
  that says it could
  not be checked.
• cch-w22-s7's
  remainder,
  finished or closed
  against
  origin/main's
  bytes rather than
  rebuilt.

THE WAVE'S OWN SIXTH
CLAUSE, PAID TWICE
MORE. Wave 27 added
it: an instrument
pointed at the wrong
ROOT reports defect-
shaped prose for a
condition that is
only “I could not
look”. Wave 28 found
the same shape in
two more places — a
test bound to an
exit code it does
not test, which
reported a DEFECT
for an ENVIRONMENT,
and a resolver
classification that
would have reported
“we could not check”
for a domain that
was simply still
propagating. The fix
in both cases was
the same move:
separate the
